\displaystyle=\,\Phi\big(\xi+\frac{1}{\varepsilon d}Z\big)\,=\,\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\log\big|\big(\xi+\frac{1}{\varepsilon d}Z\big)-\eta\big|\,f(\eta)\,{\rm d}\eta

(A.17)

\displaystyle=\,\frac{\mathrm{M}(f)}{2\pi}\,\log\Big(\frac{|Z|}{\varepsilon d}\Big)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\log\Bigl(\Big|1+\frac{\varepsilon d}{Z}(\xi-\eta)\Big|\Bigr)\,f(\eta)\,{\rm d}\eta\,.

We thus have to study the last integral. Since
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,z}\varphi\in\mathcal{S}_{*}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, it is enough to consider
\xi\in B_{r_{\varepsilon}}
with
r_{\varepsilon}:=|z_{\min}|/(4\varepsilon d)
. Indeed, for
\xi\in B_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{c}
the function is automatically of order
\varepsilon^{M+1}
. This follows because for any
f\in\mathcal{S}_{*}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
we know that there exists an
N
such that for all
|\xi|\geq r_{\varepsilon}
we have
|f(\xi)|\leq(1+|\xi|)^{N}\leq(1+|\xi|)^{N+M+1}r_{\varepsilon}^{-M-1}
, which implies that
f|_{B_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{c}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{*}}(\varepsilon^{M+1})
.
Similarly, we can restrict the domain of integration so that
\eta\in B_{r_{\varepsilon}}
,
up to negligible errors. Hence, if
\xi,\eta\in B_{r_{\varepsilon}}
, we have
|(\varepsilon d/Z)(\xi-\eta)|<1
. We can then use the expansion

(A.18)

\log|1+x|={\rm Re}\log(1+x)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n-1}}{n}\,{\rm Re}(x^{n})\,\qquad\text{ valid if }|x|<1\,,

to get

(A.19)

\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}\varphi(\xi)=\frac{\mathrm{M}(f)}{2\pi}\,\log\Big(\frac{|Z|}{\varepsilon d}\Big)+\sum_{n=1}^{M}\frac{(-1)^{n-1}}{n}\frac{(\varepsilon d)^{n}}{2\pi}\int_{B_{r_{\varepsilon}}}{\rm Re}\bigl(Z^{-1}(\xi-\eta)\bigr)^{n}\,f(\eta)\,{\rm d}\eta+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{*}}\bigl(\varepsilon^{M+1}\bigr)\

Since
f\in\mathcal{Z}
,
|Z|^{-1}\leq 1/z_{\min}
and
\xi\in B_{r_{\varepsilon}}
, we can replace
\int_{B_{r_{\varepsilon}}}
with
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
up
to an error of size
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{*}}(\varepsilon^{M+1})
, thus completing the proof for the translation operator by expanding the
n
-th power in the integral.

By the definition of
\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,Z}
and the expansion in Lemma
3.6
, we have

(A.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,Z}\varphi(\xi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{N}\mathcal{T}_{-\varepsilon,Z}(\varphi)(Q_{l}\xi)

(A.21)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{M}(\Omega)\frac{N}{2\pi}\log\Big(\frac{|Z|}{\varepsilon d}\Big)+\sum_{n=1}^{M}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{N}(-1)^{n}\varepsilon^{n}c_{n,k}{\rm Re}\Big(\frac{(Q_{l}\xi)^{n-k}}{Z^{n}}{\rm m}^{k}(f)\Big)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{*}}\bigl(\varepsilon^{M+1}\bigr)\,.

Then, we rewrite a part of the sum as

(A.22)

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{N}{\rm Re}\Big(\frac{(Q_{l}\xi)^{n-k}}{Z^{n}}{\rm m}^{k}(f)\Big)={\rm Re}\Bigg(\frac{\xi^{n-k}}{Z^{n}}\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)\sum_{l=1}^{N}Q_{l}^{n-k}\Bigg).

By the properties of the roots of unity, we know

(A.23)

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{N}Q_{l}^{n-k}=\sum_{l=1}^{N}{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi il}{N}(n-k)}=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }n-k\neq 0\,\mod N\\
N&\text{if }n-k=0\,\mod N.\end{cases}.

Therefore, we can first simplify the sum in
Eq.

A.21
by isolating the case
k=n
, which gives the second term on the right-hand side of
Lemma

3.6
. Then, we need
0\leq n-k=\ell N
with
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
since
0\leq k\leq n-1
, meaning that we have a nontrivial contribution only for
n\geq N
. Thus, summing in
\ell
instead of
k
, we finally get

(A.24)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{M}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{l=1}^{N}(-1)^{n}\varepsilon^{n}c_{n,k}{\rm Re}\Big(\frac{(Q_{l}\xi)^{n-k}}{Z^{n}}\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)\Big)=N\sum_{n=N}^{M}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}\\
\ell N\leq n\end{subarray}}(-1)^{n}\varepsilon^{n}c_{n,n-\ell N}{\rm Re}\Big(\frac{\xi^{\ell N}}{Z^{n}}\mathrm{m}^{n-\ell N}(f)\Big),

and the proof for the polygon-center operator is over.

We finally consider the operator
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}
, which is more convenient to rewrite as

(A.25)

\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}(f)=\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}f\big(Q_{l}\xi+\frac{1}{\varepsilon(t)d}Z_{l}\big),\qquad Z_{l}(t)=(Q_{l}-I)Z.

Using the expansion of
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}
, we get that

(A.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}\varphi(\xi)=\frac{\mathrm{M}(\Omega)}{2\pi}\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}\log\Big(\frac{|Z_{l}|}{\varepsilon d}\Big)+\sum_{n=1}^{M}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}\varepsilon^{n}c_{n,k}{\rm Re}\Big(\frac{(Q_{l}\xi)^{n-k}}{Z_{l}^{n}}{\rm m}^{k}(f)\Big)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{*}}\bigl(\varepsilon^{M+1}\bigr).

Recalling that
Z_{l}=(Q_{l}-1)Z
, in view of the properties of
{\rm S}_{n,k}
in Lemma
A.1
, we readily find that

(A.27)

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}{\rm Re}\Big(\frac{(Q_{l}\xi)^{n-k}}{Z_{l}^{n}}{\rm m}^{k}(f)\Big)={\rm Re}\Bigg(\frac{\xi^{n-k}}{Z^{n}}\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}\frac{Q_{l}^{n-k}}{(Q_{l}-1)^{n}}\Bigg)={\rm S}_{n,n-k}{\rm Re}\Bigg(\frac{\xi^{n-k}}{Z^{n}}\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)\Bigg),

which concludes the proof.

∎

A.3.
Expansion of the integral quantities

We now compute expansions of integral quantities involving the operators
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}
. Indeed, in view of the definitions of the radius and angular speed, these computations are necessary to rigorously establish their asymptotic expansions as stated in
Eq.

1.15
. The following lemma also establishes the expansion
Eq.

3.32
.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
f,h\in\mathcal{Z}
,
\varphi=\Delta^{-1}f
and assume that
0<z_{\min}\leq|Z|
. Then, for all
M\in\mathbb{N}
one has the expansions

(A.28)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla^{\perp}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}\varphi)h\,{\rm d}\xi=\sum_{n=1}^{M}\varepsilon^{n}\frac{Z^{\perp}}{|Z|^{2}}\frac{1}{\overline{Z^{n-1}}}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(n-k)c_{n,k}\overline{\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)}\overline{\mathrm{m}^{n-k-1}(h)}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{M+1})

and

(A.29)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla^{\perp}(\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}\varphi)h\,{\rm d}\xi=\sum_{n=1}^{M}\varepsilon^{n}\frac{Z^{\perp}}{|Z|^{2}}\frac{1}{\overline{Z^{n-1}}}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(n-k)c_{n,k}{\rm S}_{n,n-k}\overline{\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)}\overline{\mathrm{m}^{n-k-1}(h)}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{M+1}).

Proof.

Appealing to
Lemma

A.2
, we know that

(A.30)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}\varphi=\sum_{n=1}^{M}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\varepsilon^{n}c_{n,k}\nabla^{\perp}{\rm Re}\bigg(\frac{\xi^{n-k}}{Z^{n}}\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)\bigg)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{*}}(\varepsilon^{M+1}).

To compute the orthogonal gradient, we pass to polar coordinates
\xi=r{\rm e}^{i\theta}
(keeping the complex number notation) and we note that

(A.31)

\nabla^{\perp}=i({\partial}_{1}+i{\partial}_{2})\to i{\rm e}^{i\theta}({\partial}_{r}+i\frac{1}{r}{\partial}_{\theta}).

Then, denoting

(A.32)

\displaystyle Z=|Z|{\rm e}^{i\phi_{Z}},\qquad\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)=|\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)|{\rm e}^{i\phi_{k}},

we get

(A.33)

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\bigg(\frac{\xi^{n-k}}{Z^{n}}\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)\bigg)\to\frac{|\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)|}{2|Z|^{n}}r^{n-k}\big({\rm e}^{i(n-k)\theta+i(\phi_{k}-n\phi_{Z})}+{\rm e}^{-i(n-k)\theta-i(\phi_{k}-n\phi_{Z})}\big).

Thus, applying the
\nabla^{\perp}
we see that the right-hand side becomes

(A.34)

\displaystyle i(n-k)\frac{|\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)|}{2|Z|^{n}}r^{n-k-1}\big({\rm e}^{i(n-k+1)\theta+i(\phi_{k}-n\phi_{Z})}+{\rm e}^{-i(n-k-1)\theta-i(\phi_{k}-n\phi_{Z})}\big)

(A.35)

\displaystyle-i(n-k)\frac{|\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)|}{2|Z|^{n}}r^{n-k-1}\big({\rm e}^{i(n-k+1)\theta+i(\phi_{k}-n\phi_{Z})}-{\rm e}^{-i(n-k-1)\theta-i(\phi_{k}-n\phi_{Z})}\big)

(A.36)

\displaystyle=i(n-k)\frac{|\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)|}{|Z|^{n}}r^{n-k-1}{\rm e}^{-i(n-k-1)\theta-i(\phi_{k}-n\phi_{Z})}.

Going back to Cartesian coordinates, note that

(A.37)

\displaystyle r^{n-k-1}{\rm e}^{-i(n-k-1)\theta}\to\overline{\xi^{n-k-1}},\qquad|\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)|{\rm e}^{-i\phi_{k}}\to\overline{\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)}

and

(A.38)

\displaystyle i\frac{1}{|Z|^{n}}{\rm e}^{in\phi_{Z}}=i\frac{|Z|}{|Z|^{n+1}}{\rm e}^{in\phi_{Z}}\to\frac{Z^{\perp}}{|Z|^{2}}\frac{1}{\overline{Z^{n-1}}}.

Therefore, we have computed that

(A.39)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}{\rm Re}\bigg(\frac{\xi^{n-k}}{Z^{n}}\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)\bigg)=(n-k)\frac{Z^{\perp}}{|Z|^{2}}\frac{1}{\overline{Z^{n-1}}}\overline{\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)}\overline{\xi^{n-k-1}}.

Since
h\in\mathcal{Z}
, we then easily deduce that

(A.40)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\nabla^{\perp}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}\varphi)h\,{\rm d}\xi=\sum_{n=1}^{M}\varepsilon^{n}\frac{Z^{\perp}}{|Z|^{2}}\frac{1}{\overline{Z^{n-1}}}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(n-k)c_{n,k}\overline{\mathrm{m}^{k}(f)}\overline{\mathrm{m}^{n-k-1}(h)}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{M+1}),

which proves
Eq.

A.28
.
The proof for
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}
is now straightforward using the same arguments.
∎

A.4.
Commutators and adjoints

We now prove Lemma
3.9
. Most of the properties hold for any vector
Z
, but we rely on the specific choice
Z=(R,0)
to prove the preservation of parity in
\xi_{2}
for
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}
. Therefore, we present the proof for a general
Z
, explicitly restricting to
Z=(R,0)
only when necessary.

Proof of Lemma
3.9
.

The identity for
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}^{*}
is a direct computation using the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}
. Regarding the commutation property with
\Delta
, we simply exploit that a translation commutes with spatial derivatives.

By the definition of
\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,Z}
, since
Q_{l}
are rotation matrices we have

(A.41)

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,Z}g,f\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{N}g\big(Q_{l}\xi-\frac{1}{\varepsilon d}Z\big)f(\xi){\rm d}\xi

(A.42)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g\big(\eta)\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{N}f\big(Q_{-l}(\eta+\frac{1}{\varepsilon d}Z)\big)\Big){\rm d}\xi=\langle g,\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,Z}^{*}f\rangle.

The commutation properties with
\Delta
follow by the fact that
\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,Z},\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,Z}^{*}
are compositions with a rotation operator
({\sf R}f)(\xi)=f(Q_{l}\xi)
and translations
{\sf T}(f)=f(\xi+z)
for a fixed vector
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Indeed, since clearly
[{\sf R},\Delta]=0
and
[{\sf T},\Delta]=0
then also
[\sf{R}\sf{T},\Delta]=[\sf{T}\sf{R},\Delta]=0
as desired.
The property about
N
-fold symmetric functions is a direct consequence of the first identity in
Eq.

3.26
.

For
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}^{*}
, it is enough to observe that

(A.43)

Q_{l}\xi+\frac{1}{\varepsilon d}Z_{l}=\eta\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\xi=Q_{-\ell}\eta+\frac{1}{\varepsilon d}Z_{-l},

which readily imply that
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}
is self-adjoint in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
by the identification of
Q_{-l}=Q_{N-l}
. The commutation property with the Laplacian follows since
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}
is a combination of translations and rotations. Finally, the property for even functions can be seen as follows: identify
(\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})
with the complex conjugation
\overline{\xi}
,
Z
with the real number
R
and
Q_{l}=\exp(2\pi il/N)
. Then, observe that

(A.44)

\displaystyle Q_{N-l}\overline{\xi}+(\varepsilon d)^{-1}(Q_{N-l}-1)R=Q_{-l}\overline{\xi}+(\varepsilon d)^{-1}(Q_{-l}-1)R=\overline{Q_{l}\xi+(\varepsilon d)^{-1}(Q_{l}-1)R}.

Hence, if
f
is even we can combine the identity above with the fact that we are summing over
l=1,\dots,N-1
to conclude the proof.

It remains to show
Eqs.

3.27
,
3.29
and
3.28
. First of all, we denote
Z_{\varepsilon}=Z/(\varepsilon d)
and we introduce

(A.45)

\eta_{l}(\xi)=Q_{l}(\xi+Z_{\varepsilon})-Z_{\varepsilon}.

In this way, we can rewrite

(A.46)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}f(\xi)=\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}f(\eta_{l}(\xi))

Then we observe that

(A.47)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\{|\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}\xi|^{2},f\}=(\xi+Z_{\varepsilon})^{\perp}\cdot\nabla f.

Thus

(A.48)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}\{|\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}\xi|^{2},f\})(\xi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}(\eta_{l}(\xi)+Z_{\varepsilon})^{\perp}\cdot(\nabla f)(\eta_{l}(\xi))

(A.49)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}(Q_{l}(\xi+Z_{\varepsilon}))^{\perp}\cdot(\nabla f)(\eta_{l}(\xi)),

where in the last identity we used the definition of
\eta_{l}(\xi).
On the other hand

(A.50)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\{|\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}\xi|^{2},\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}f\}

\displaystyle=(\xi+Z_{\varepsilon})^{\perp}\cdot\nabla(\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,Z}f)=\sum_{l=1}^{N}(\xi+Z_{\varepsilon})^{\perp}\cdot\nabla(f(\eta_{l}(\xi)))

(A.51)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{N}(\xi+Z_{\varepsilon})^{\perp}\cdot Q_{l}^{T}(\nabla f)(\eta_{l}(\xi)).

Then, by the property of the dot product
v\cdot(M^{T}w)=Mv\cdot w
and
Q_{l}v^{\perp}=(Q_{l}v)^{\perp}
since
Q_{l}
is a rotation matrix, we see that indeed
Eq.

3.27
holds true by combining
Eq.

A.49
with
Eq.

A.51
.

To prove
Eq.

3.29
, we denote
y_{l}(\xi)=Q_{l}(\xi+Z_{\varepsilon})
and, in view of
Eq.

2.38
, we have that

(A.52)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,R}^{*}(\{|\xi|^{2},f\})=\sum_{l=1}^{N}y_{l}(\xi)^{\perp}\cdot(\nabla f)(y_{l}(\xi)).

Instead

(A.53)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\{|\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon,Z}\xi|^{2},\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,R}^{*}f\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{N}(\xi+Z_{\varepsilon})^{\perp}\cdot\nabla(f(y_{l}(\xi)))=\sum_{l=1}^{N}(\xi+Z_{\varepsilon})^{\perp}\cdot Q_{l}^{T}(\nabla f)(y_{l}(\xi))

(A.54)

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{N}(Q_{l}(\xi+Z_{\varepsilon}))^{\perp}\cdot(\nabla f)(y_{l}(\xi))=\sum_{l=1}^{N}y_{l}(\xi)^{\perp}\cdot(\nabla f)(y_{l}(\xi)),

meaning that
Eq.

3.29
is proved.
Finally,
Eq.

3.28
directly follows by taking the
L^{2}
adjoint of the identity in
Eq.

3.29
(or it can be proved directly as before).
∎

Appendix B
Viscous deviations from the point-vortex dynamics

Let the family of approximate solutions be constructed as in Section
4
. We establish the following result, which provides the necessary tools to compute the first-order perturbations to
R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}
and
\alpha^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}
relative to the point-vortex dynamics.

Lemma B.1
.

For every
M\geq 5
and
\varepsilon
small enough, there holds that

(B.1)

\displaystyle{\rm Im}(

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}))=\frac{\varepsilon}{R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}c_{1,0}(S_{1,1}+\gamma)

(B.2)

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon^{5}}{(R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}})^{3}}\Big(3c_{3,0}({\rm S}_{3,3}+\gamma)\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega_{2})+c_{3,2}({\rm S}_{3,1}\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega_{2})+\gamma\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}_{2})\big)\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{6}+\delta\varepsilon^{5})

and

(B.3)

{\rm Re}(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}))\\
=-\delta\frac{\varepsilon^{5}}{(R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}})^{3}}\Big(3c_{3,0}\big({\rm S}_{3,3}+\gamma\big)\mathrm{m}_{2}^{2}(\Omega_{2,\mathrm{NS}})+c_{3,2}\big({\rm S}_{3,1}\mathrm{m}_{2}^{2}(\Omega_{2,\mathrm{NS}})+\gamma\mathrm{m}_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}_{2,\mathrm{NS}})\big)\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\delta\varepsilon^{6}).

Proof.

We first split the Euler and Navier-Stokes part, with the cross-interactions being denoted with the three-variable definition of
\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}
:

(B.4)

\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}})=\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})+\delta\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})\\
+\delta\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}},\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})+\delta^{2}\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}}).

We recall the expansion
Eq.

3.32
to be:

(B.5)

\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\varphi,\widehat{\varphi},\widetilde{\varphi})=i\sum_{n=1}^{M}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}\right)^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(n-k)c_{n,k}\left({\rm S}_{n,n-k}\overline{\mathrm{m}^{k}(w)}+\gamma\overline{\mathrm{m}^{k}(\widehat{w})}\right)\overline{\mathrm{m}^{n-k-1}(\widetilde{w})}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{M+1}),

Now, we recall from the construction of the first orders that

(B.6)

\displaystyle\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}),\quad\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}),

(B.7)

\displaystyle\mathrm{M}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})=\mathrm{M}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})=0,

(B.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{m}^{1}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})=\mathrm{m}^{1}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=\mathrm{m}^{1}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})=\mathrm{m}^{1}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=0.

We deduce from plugging these identities into the expansion
Eq.

B.5
that

(B.9)

\delta^{2}\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}},\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{2}(\varepsilon^{9}+\varepsilon^{M+1})),

since to produce no vanishing terms, it must hold that
n-k-1\geq 2
and
k\geq 2
. This can happen only if
n\geq 5
, while we also have two factors
\varepsilon^{2}
from
\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}}
itself. Following the same reasoning, since
M\geq 5
, we have that

(B.10)

\delta\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})=\mathcal{O}(\delta\varepsilon^{5}),\quad\delta\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}},\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=\mathcal{O}(\delta\varepsilon^{5})

with 3 powers of
\varepsilon
coming from
\varepsilon^{n}
and 2 from
\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}}
or
\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}}
.

We now compute the imaginary part of the purely Eulerian part
\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})
. To obtain an expansion of this expression, we plug into (
B.5
) the fact that
\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}}
and
\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}}
are even in
\xi_{2}
by construction (constraint
C2)
), meaning that for every
k\geq 1
,

(B.11)

\mathrm{m}^{k}_{2}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=\mathrm{m}^{k}_{2}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=0.

This gives the following expansion:

(B.12)

{\rm Im}(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}}))\\
=\sum_{n=1}^{M}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}\right)^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(n-k)c_{n,k}\Big(S_{n,n-k}\mathrm{m}_{1}^{k}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})+\gamma\mathrm{m}_{1}^{k}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})\Big)\mathrm{m}_{1}^{n-k-1}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{M+1}).

We now compute the first terms in that expression. The
n=1
term is non vanishing, equal to
c_{1,0}(S_{1,1}+\gamma)
since

(B.13)

\mathrm{M}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=\mathrm{M}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=1,

and is the term giving the point-vortex dynamics angular velocity as the first-order value. The
n=2
terms vanish due to the facts that the first moments vanish, as in that case, either
k=1
or
n-k-1=1
. We now turn to the case
n=3
, computing for each value of
k
the terms in the sums:

(B.14)

\displaystyle k=0\quad\rightarrow\quad 3c_{3,0}\Big({\rm S}_{3,3}+\gamma\Big)\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}}),

(B.15)

\displaystyle k=1\quad\rightarrow\quad 0

(B.16)

\displaystyle k=2\quad\rightarrow\quad c_{3,2}\Big({\rm S}_{3,1}\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})+\gamma\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})\Big).

Since
\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})
and
\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})
, these terms give rise to terms of order
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{5})
in
{\rm Im}(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}}))
. Let us now observe that there are no other lower-order terms. For
n=4
, since we already have
4
powers due to
\varepsilon^{n}
, to obtain a term of order 5 or less it would require a contribution from the moments of order at most 1. However,
\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}}=\Omega_{0}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})
and
\Omega_{0}
has only vanishing moments except
\mathrm{M}(\Omega_{0})
. Since
k=0
and
n-k-1=0
can not be realized at the same time, no such term can exist. The same applies to the terms
n=5
, and higher terms are at least of order
\varepsilon^{n}
. In conclusion, we have shown, using in addition that
\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=\varepsilon^{2}\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega_{2})+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{3}+\delta\varepsilon^{2})
and
\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})=\varepsilon^{2}\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}_{2})+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{3}+\delta\varepsilon^{2})
, that

(B.17)

{\rm Im}(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}}))=\frac{\varepsilon}{R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}c_{1,0}(S_{1,1}+\gamma)\\
+\frac{\varepsilon^{5}}{(R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}})^{3}}\Big(3c_{3,0}({\rm S}_{3,3}+\gamma)\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega_{2})+c_{3,2}({\rm S}_{3,1}\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega_{2})+\gamma\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}_{2})\big)\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{6}+\delta\varepsilon^{5}).

Gathering this expansion with the estimates
Eqs.

B.10
and
B.9
, gives that

(B.18)

{\rm Im}(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}))=\frac{\varepsilon}{R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}c_{1,0}(S_{1,1}+\gamma)\\
+\frac{\varepsilon^{5}}{(R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}})^{3}}\Big(3c_{3,0}({\rm S}_{3,3}+\gamma)\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega_{2})+c_{3,2}({\rm S}_{3,1}\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\Omega_{2})+\gamma\mathrm{m}_{1}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}_{2})\big)\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{6}+\delta\varepsilon^{5}).

Notice that applying the expansions for
M\geq 5
is actually necessary to conclude with that precision, although all terms of order
\leq 5
are induced only by
\Omega_{2}
and
\widehat{\Omega}_{2}
.

We now turn to the real part of
\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}})
. By Lemma
3.11
, the purely Eulerian term has no real part. To compute the first terms of the expansion, one thus has to look to a more precise expansion of
Eq.

B.10
. Using again relation
Eq.

B.11
into
Eq.

B.5
, we compute that

(B.19)

{\rm Re}\Big(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}},\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})+\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}},\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})\Big)\\
=-\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}\right)^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(n-k)c_{n,k}\bigg[\big(S_{n,n-k}\mathrm{m}_{1}^{k}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})+\gamma\mathrm{m}_{1}^{k}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})\big)\mathrm{m}_{2}^{n-k-1}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})\\
+\big(S_{n,n-k}\mathrm{m}_{2}^{k}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})+\gamma\mathrm{m}_{2}^{k}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})\big)\mathrm{m}_{1}^{n-k-1}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,E}})\bigg]+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{M+1}).

To produce non vanishing terms, one needs in the first part of the sum that
n-k-1\geq 2
, namely
n\geq 3
, and in the second part that
k\geq 2
, which again, requires
n\geq 3
. We thus compute the terms
n=3
, which are of order
\varepsilon^{5}
:

(B.20)

\displaystyle k=0\quad\rightarrow\quad 3c_{3,0}\Big({\rm S}_{3,3}+\gamma\Big)\mathrm{m}_{2}^{2}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}}),

(B.21)

\displaystyle k=1\quad\rightarrow\quad 0

(B.22)

\displaystyle k=2\quad\rightarrow\quad c_{3,2}\Big({\rm S}_{3,1}\mathrm{m}_{2}^{2}(\Omega^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})+\gamma\mathrm{m}_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app,NS}})\Big).

Terms with
n\geq 4
are of order at least
\varepsilon^{6}
due to the presence of Navier-Stokes pieces. In conclusion,

(B.23)

{\rm Re}(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon,R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}}(\Psi^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}},\widehat{\Psi}^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}}))\\
=\delta\frac{\varepsilon^{5}}{(R^{(M)}_{\mathrm{app}})^{3}}\Big(3c_{3,0}\big({\rm S}_{3,3}+\gamma\big)\mathrm{m}_{2}^{2}(\Omega_{2,\mathrm{NS}})+c_{3,2}\big({\rm S}_{3,1}\mathrm{m}_{2}^{2}(\Omega_{2,\mathrm{NS}})+\gamma\mathrm{m}_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}_{2,\mathrm{NS}})\big)\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\delta\varepsilon^{6}).

∎
